It’s the kind of tired that doesn’t seem to go away with sleep, and even getting through a normal workday and then making it through dinner at home feels a lot heavier than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loss of interest or pleasure in activities that you usually enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. I usually really enjoy painting, and I like going to museum exhibits just to wander around, but lately I haven’t touched my supplies. Even the idea of going to a museum feels like too much, and I just can’t seem to care enough to make myself go.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the day already feels disappointing before it’s even started. It’s not dramatic, exactly, but it’s there a lot of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the low mood and loss of interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a month, I think. I had a period where I felt unusually energized and really driven, and then it shifted into this low mood and not caring about things. That low part has been sticking around since then.\n\nDoctor: Has the low mood and lack of interest in things you used to enjoy been present most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been more than two weeks — probably around a month now. Since that energized, driven period ended, I’ve mostly been feeling low and not interested in things most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period like this before, where you felt low and lost interest in things, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this isn’t the first time. I’ve had stretches like this before. The longest one I can clearly remember lasted about five months, where I felt low, tired, and just not like myself most days.\n\nDoctor: During your previous low mood periods, did you ever experience a complete remission where you felt back to your usual self for at least a couple of months before this most recent episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think there were times I felt more like myself again, but I don’t remember clearly whether it lasted a full couple of months before this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two separate periods in the past where you felt this same kind of low mood and loss of interest, lasting for weeks or months at a time?\n\nPatient: The one I remember most clearly lasted about five months. I was feeling low and tired and just not like myself most days. I’m not sure I can clearly count how many separate times, but that longer stretch definitely happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time between your previous low mood periods when you felt back to your usual self for at least two months before another episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know there were stretches where I felt more normal again, but I can’t clearly remember whether it was a full two months before things got worse again.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a period of at least two months between your previous low mood episodes where you felt completely back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: I can’t really confirm that. I remember feeling more like myself at times, but I don’t know if it was completely back to normal for a full two months between episodes.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if there was ever a time when you were completely free of these symptoms for at least two months between your previous low mood episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember a clear two-month stretch where I was completely free of it. I had times that were better, but I can’t say for sure it was totally gone for that long.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Even the idea of going to a museum feels like too much, and I just can’t seem to care enough to make myself go.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the day already feels disappointing before it’s even started. It’s not dramatic, exactly, but it’s there a lot of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the low mood and loss of interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a month, I think. I had a period where I felt unusually energized and really driven, and then it shifted into this low mood and not caring about things. That low part has been sticking around since then.\n\nDoctor: Has the low mood and lack of interest in things you used to enjoy been present most of the day, nearly every day, for the past two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s been more than two weeks — probably around a month now. Since that energized, driven period ended, I’ve mostly been feeling low and not interested in things most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period like this before, where you felt low and lost interest in things, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, this isn’t the first time. I’ve had stretches like this before. The longest one I can clearly remember lasted about five months, where I felt low, tired, and just not like myself most days.\n\nDoctor: During your previous low mood periods, did you ever experience a complete remission where you felt back to your usual self for at least a couple of months before this most recent episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure. I think there were times I felt more like myself again, but I don’t remember clearly whether it lasted a full couple of months before this started.\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two separate periods in the past where you felt this same kind of low mood and loss of interest, lasting for weeks or months at a time?\n\nPatient: The one I remember most clearly lasted about five months. I was feeling low and tired and just not like myself most days. I’m not sure I can clearly count how many separate times, but that longer stretch definitely happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time between your previous low mood periods when you felt back to your usual self for at least two months before another episode started?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know there were stretches where I felt more normal again, but I can’t clearly remember whether it was a full two months before things got worse again.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a period of at least two months between your previous low mood episodes where you felt completely back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: I can’t really confirm that. I remember feeling more like myself at times, but I don’t know if it was completely back to normal for a full two months between episodes.\n\nDoctor: Can you remember if there was ever a time when you were completely free of these symptoms for at least two months between your previous low mood episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t really remember a clear two-month stretch where I was completely free of it. I had times that were better, but I can’t say for sure it was totally gone for that long.\n\nDoctor: Before this most recent episode, can you confirm if you had at least one other distinct period in your life when you experienced a low mood and loss of interest that lasted for weeks or months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. The clearest one I remember lasted around five months. I felt low and tired most days, and just not like myself for that stretch.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if you’ve had at least two separate times in the past when you felt this low and lost interest in things, with a break of at least two months in between where you felt completely back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can confirm that. I do remember more than one low period, but I can’t clearly say there were two separate ones with a full two months in between where I felt completely back to my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a time when you had two separate low mood episodes in the past, each lasting weeks or months, with at least a two-month period in between where you felt completely back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: I can’t really confirm that clearly. What I can say is this recent shift—from feeling unusually energized and driven to being more persistently low—has been going on for about a month.\n\nDoctor: Can you confirm if there was a time in the past when you had two separate low mood episodes, each lasting weeks or months, where you felt completely back to your usual self for at least two months in between?\n\nPatient: I still can’t confirm that.
